This can happen when a component repeatedly calls setState inside componentWillUpdate or componentDidUpdate. React limits the number of  \r\n  nested updates to prevent infinite loops.                                                                                                                                      \r\n      at setRef (composeRefs.tsx:11:5)                                                                                                                                           \r\n      at composeRefs.tsx:22:45                                                                                                                                                   \r\n      at Array.forEach                                                                                                                                       \r\n      at setRef (composeRefs.tsx:11:5)                                                                                               \r\n      ...                                                                                                                                                                        \r\n                                                                                                                                                                                 \r\n  The setRef function in @radix-ui/react-compose-refs recursively triggers itself, causing React to hit the maximum update depth limit.                                          \r\n                                                                                                                                                                                 \r\n  In React 19, ref callback behavior changed - ref callbacks can now return cleanup functions, and React may call ref callbacks differently than in React 18. When setRef calls  \r\n  ref(value) at line 11, it triggers a re-render which invokes setRef again via the composed ref callback at line 22, creating an infinite loop.    \r\n\r\n\r\n### Expected behavior\r\n\r\n  Composed refs should work without causing infinite update loops in React 19.                                                                                                    \r\n\r\n### Reproducible example\r\n\r\nI have platejs plugins with radix popovers and radix tooltips on them. If I write fast inside plate, the page breaks. \r\nI\'m on a Macbook M1 Pro with 32 GB. One way to trigger this error is to inspect chrome and start a performance recording and start typing real fast. On Safari, for example, it doesn\'t break. I tried on restarted browser, guest mode. I\'m not the only one\r\n\r\n[CodeSandbox Template](n/a)\r\n\r\n### Suggested solution\r\n\r\n  The composeRefs and useComposedRefs functions need to handle React 19\'s new ref callback behavior. Possible approaches:                                                        \r\n                                                                                                                                                                                 \r\n  1. Memoize the composed ref callback to prevent unnecessary re-invocations                                                                                                     \r\n  2. Add a guard to prevent recursive setRef calls                                                                                                                               \r\n  3. Handle the cleanup function return value from React 19 ref callbacks       \r\n\r\n### Additional context\r\nRight now, it works fine with react 18 (even on next.js 16+) and we had to downgrade.\r\n\r\nI also saw this reddit post https://www.reddit.com/r/react/comments/1nnvwsp/react_19_causes_maximum_update_depth_exceeded/ where a user raised a similar case\r\n\r\nReact 19 introduced changes to ref callback behavior, including support for cleanup functions returned from ref callbacks. This may be causing the ref to be re-invoked on each render cycle.                                                                                                                                                                 \r\n {'role': 'assistant', 'content': [{'type': 'thinking', 'thinking': 'The skipped test "should call the function ref only once" — this is a known issue! Radix PR #3554 reverted the memoization of the composed ref. The nature of the bug: when using `asChild`, the ref callback is called multiple times (once per render) — in React 19.2, when the identity of the callback ref changes, React calls the old one with null and the new one with the node. In the case of react-hook-form / slate (plate) input, the ref callback registers the input, and calling it again with node triggers `setState` (registration) → re-render → new ref identity → ref is called again → setState → infinite loop → "Maximum update depth exceeded".\n\nSo the fix is: memoize the composed ref callback in Slot so it stays stable across renders (useComposedRefs) — only recreate it when forwardedRef or slottableElementRef changes. In other words, use `useComposedRefs` from @radix-ui/react-compose-refs instead of a plain `composeRefs` inside Slot.\n\nWait, but `useComposedRefs(...refs)` with `refs` as the dependency array — the dependency array is an array of refs created every render... `React.useCallback(composeRefs(...refs), refs)` — passing an array as the dependency array means a new array every render → no memoization! Hmm, actually passing an array literal as a dependency array means new identity every render, so useCallback re-creates every render. So useComposedRefs as written also provides no stability... no, actually — `React.useCallback(fn, refs)` when `refs` is a new array each render → new callback every render. So useComposedRefs is useless unless the caller memoizes the refs array.\n\nHmm, but the real fix in the upstream radix is... let me check what upstream did. There\'s a recent (2025) fix in the actual radix-ui repo for exactly this React 19 "Maximum update depth exceeded" issue. Let me check the .changeset files in this repo — a changeset for the fix might already be included. {'role': 'assistant', 'content': [{'type': 'thinking', 'thinking': 'Complete. Report: root cause, files changed, verification.\n', 'thinkingSignature': 'reasoning'}, {'type': 'text', 'text': '\n\nDone. All 31 slot tests pass (2 pre-existing skips), typecheck and lint clean.\n\n## Root cause\n\n`Slot` (`packages/react/slot/src/slot.tsx`) built the ref passed to the cloned child with a **plain `composeRefs(...)` call inside the render function**, so every render produced a *new* ref callback identity. React detaches the old callback ref (call with `null`) and re-attaches the new one (call with the node) on every commit. When the ref callback performs side effects that schedule updates — Slate/Plate editor registration, react-hook-form `register`, any `setState`-on-attach — each re-attach triggers a render, which creates another fresh ref, which re-attaches, … → **`Maximum update depth exceeded`**, with the stack pointing at `setRef` in `compose-refs`. Reproduced exactly (same error, same stack frames) before the fix; typing fast in a Plate editor is just the fast re-render trigger.\n\n`composeRefs` itself was fine (correct composition + React 19 cleanup handling); the bug was the unstable identity in `Slot`.\n\n## Files changed\n\n- `packages/react/slot/src/slot.tsx` — import `useComposedRefs` instead of `composeRefs`; compute `slottableElementRef` before the early return (hook rule) and memoize: `const composedRefs = useComposedRefs(forwardedRef, slottableElementRef)`. `useComposedRefs` caches via `useCallback` keyed on the actual ref values, so the composed ref identity is stable across renders and only changes when a real ref changes.\n- `packages/react/slot/src/slot.test.tsx` — new `describe(\'Slot ref stability across renders\')` regression block: (1) a stable ref callback is invoked exactly once across re-renders; (2) a ref callback that `setState`s on attach no longer trips "Maximum update depth exceeded". Both failed before the fix (test 2 with the exact reported error).\n- `.changeset/tidy-slots-loop.md` — patch changeset, per repo convention.\n\n## Verification\n\n- Repro first: scratch test reproduced `Error: Maximum update depth exceeded … at setRef (compose-refs.tsx:11)` before the fix; removed after porting the assertions into `slot.test.tsx`.\n- `pnpm vitest run packages/react/slot`: 31 passed / 2 skipped (the 2 skips are the pre-existing `describe.skip(\'given an Input\')` block, untouched).\n- `pnpm typecheck` and `pnpm lint` in `packages/react/slot`: clean.'}], 'api': 'openai-completions', 'provider': 'pod-qwen-medium', 'model': 'qwen3.8-27b-bf16', 'usage': {'input': 35538, 'output': 585, 'cacheRead': 0, 'cacheWrite': 0, 'totalTokens': 36123, 'cost': {'input': 0, 'output': 0, 'cacheRead': 0, 'cacheWrite': 0, 'total': 0}}, 'stopReason': 'stop', 'timestamp': 1787269432522, 'responseId': 'chatcmpl-b6dbc197f09c6507', 'duration': 18229.129399999976, 'ttft': 8253.674100000062, 'contextSnapshot': {'promptTokens': 35538, 'nonMessageTokens': 6752}}
